### Part-level ratings and notes

#### Part 1: Investment banking and advisory

Rating key: **strong** = routinely hired or the modal profile; **possible** = hired regularly but you need extra finance preparation or a stronger internship record; **stretch** = rare; needs a deliberate route (extra degree, relevant internships, or an adjacent role first).

#### (a) Ratings by background

| Background | Rating | One-line reason |
|---|---|---|
| Management | Possible | Business degrees feed IB graduate programmes, but you need to prove technical depth (accounting, valuation) through coursework, a finance master's or internships |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | Stretch | Little direct overlap; the best bridge is trade finance and transaction banking or industrials/transport coverage, plus a finance master's or internship |
| Finance | Strong | The modal profile for IB analysts and for all six role families |
| Accounting | Strong | Excellent for transaction services and restructuring (accounting and cash analysis), and respected in M&A and credit roles |
| Marketing | Stretch | Weak technical signal; viable only with a finance minor, strong modelling skills and relevant internships, or via corporate banking graduate schemes |
| Data Analytics | Possible | Strong Excel and data skills; need accounting and valuation knowledge; good fit for Big 4 deals and some credit roles |
| Economics | Strong | Historically the largest feeder degree for IB (2016-17 analyst-class data) and fits every role family |
| Computer Science | Possible | Wanted by banks in technology roles and for technology coverage; for IBD front office you need finance prep and internships; whole-bank STEM intake is rising but IBD less so |
| Cybersecurity | Stretch | Little overlap with deal work; routes exist via technology-sector coverage or tech roles in banks, which are outside this report |
| Data Science | Possible | Quantitative strength helps modelling and credit; needs accounting and finance coursework; many STEM candidates add a finance master's |
| Artificial Intelligence | Stretch | Valued in quant and engineering parts of finance (other parts of this branch); banks are also automating junior IBD work, so front-office demand for this profile is limited |

#### (b) Fit matrix by role family

S = strong, P = possible, X = stretch.

| Background | 3.1 M&A and coverage | 3.2 ECM | 3.3 DCM / LevFin | 3.4 Restructuring | 3.5 Corporate and transaction banking | 3.6 Transaction services / valuation / CF advisory |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Management | P | P | P | P | S | P |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | X | X | X | X | P | X |
| Finance | S | S | S | S | S | S |
| Accounting | P | P | P | S | P | S |
| Marketing | X | X | X | X | P | X |
| Data Analytics | P | P | P | P | P | P |
| Economics | S | S | S | P | S | P |
| Computer Science | P | P | P | X | P | X |
| Cybersecurity | X | X | X | X | X | X |
| Data Science | P | P | P | P | P | X |
| Artificial Intelligence | X | X | X | X | X | X |

Notes: ratings are my judgement from the cited evidence (employer pages and press), not outcome statistics; none of the banks publishes IBD-only degree splits after 2017 (project earlier file); a finance master's or a strong internship can raise a "possible" to "strong" for most rows; candidates from any background should check visa and language requirements for their target hub.

#### Part 2: Sales & trading, markets and research

#### (a) Fit by background

| Background | Rating | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Management | Stretch | Weak in quant and market content; fits sales or the commodity operations route only if you add finance/maths and market knowledge. |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | Possible | A direct feeder for physical commodity houses (shipping, scheduling, operations) but stretch for bank desks. |
| Finance | Strong | The mainstream feeder for bank S&T, research and sales, and a base for lateral moves to hedge funds. |
| Accounting | Possible | Strong for equity research (models, financial statements) and credit analysis; weaker for trading and structuring. |
| Marketing | Stretch | Valuable communication skills but few quantitative signals; sales is the only plausible role without extra study. |
| Data Analytics | Possible | Helpful for desks and commodity analytics; needs markets and derivatives knowledge to compete for trader seats. |
| Economics | Strong | Natural for rates/FX, macro hedge funds, research and commodity market analysis. |
| Computer Science | Possible | Strong for electronic trading and quant roles (cross-reference the quant part); bank voice-trading and sales seats still need market knowledge. |
| Cybersecurity | Stretch | No direct fit to these front-office roles; relevant to trading technology and infrastructure, which are covered elsewhere. |
| Data Science | Possible | Strong on quant desks and for structuring, strats and commodity analytics; market knowledge must be added. |
| Artificial Intelligence | Possible | Employers (Johnson Associates 2026) say AI skills command premiums, but trader/sales seats still demand markets knowledge; best fit with quant/e-trading roles. |

#### (b) Matrix (S = strong, P = possible, X = stretch)

| Background | 3.1 Bank trader | 3.2 Salesperson | 3.3 Structurer | 3.4 Research | 3.5 Commodity house | 3.6 HF discretionary PM |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Management | X | P | X | X | P | X |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | X | X | X | P | S | X |
| Finance | S | S | P | S | P | P |
| Accounting | X | P | X | S | P | X |
| Marketing | X | P | X | X | X | X |
| Data Analytics | P | P | P | P | P | X |
| Economics | S | S | P | S | S | S |
| Computer Science | S | P | P | P | P | P |
| Cybersecurity | X | X | X | X | X | X |
| Data Science | S | P | S | P | S | P |
| Artificial Intelligence | P | X | P | P | P | X |

Notes: (1) The matrix is judgement based on the employer requirements found (for instance, Gunvor lists trading, engineering, maths, statistics, data science as eligible degrees; Trafigura's Development programme targets operations and trade finance), not a statistical outcome. (2) In the hedge fund column almost everyone enters laterally from the sell side, so the rating reflects the undergraduate route to the sell-side seat that feeds it.

#### Part 3: The buy side

#### (a) Fit by background

| Background | Fit | One-line reason |
|---|---|---|
| Management | Possible | Good for distribution, private banking and operator-turned-VC; weak for modelling-heavy roles unless paired with a finance track |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | Stretch | Rarely a direct feeder; useful for asset-based lending, supply-chain-focused credit, or sector-specialist VC/PE |
| Finance | Strong | The default feeder for every role family, from asset management to PE and private banking |
| Accounting | Possible | Strong for credit, due diligence and PE via transaction services; weaker on the market-views side |
| Marketing | Stretch | Helpful in product and distribution or consumer-sector VC; investing roles need finance proof |
| Data Analytics | Possible | Valued for alternative data, risk, and performance analytics; still needs financial literacy |
| Economics | Strong | Fits macro, allocators and asset management research; strong for pension and SWF roles |
| Computer Science | Possible | Strong for systematic or data roles (quant part), fintech and deep-tech VC; fundamental investing needs finance learning |
| Cybersecurity | Stretch | Niche: cyber-focused VC or technical diligence only |
| Data Science | Possible | Pod platforms and large managers hire data teams; fundamental roles are a stretch without finance credentials |
| Artificial Intelligence | Possible | In demand for AI-focused VC/growth and data research at funds; investing roles still require financial training |

#### (b) Matrix (S = strong, P = possible, X = stretch)

| Background | 3.1 AM invest | 3.2 AM distr. | 3.3 Hedge fund | 3.4 PE | 3.5 VC | 3.6 Private credit | 3.7 Allocator | 3.8 Private banker |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Management | X | P | X | X | P | X | X | P |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | X | X | X | X | P | P | X | X |
| Finance | S | S | S | S | P | S | S | S |
| Accounting | P | P | P | P | P | S | P | P |
| Marketing | X | P | X | X | P | X | X | P |
| Data Analytics | P | P | P | X | P | P | P | X |
| Economics | S | P | S | P | P | P | S | P |
| Computer Science | P | X | P | X | P | X | X | X |
| Cybersecurity | X | X | X | X | P | X | X | X |
| Data Science | P | X | P | X | P | X | P | X |
| Artificial Intelligence | P | X | P | X | P | X | X | X |

Note: "S" on a role family means the background is a standard feeder, not that entry is easy; PE, hedge fund and VC remain extremely selective for everyone (entry difficulty 5/5), and in PE the realistic route for most backgrounds is through banking, consulting or transaction services first.

#### Part 4: Corporate finance, risk, quant and fintech

#### (a) Fit by base background

| Background | Fit | One-line reason |
|---|---|---|
| Management | possible | Good for fintech business roles and corp dev ideas, but corporate finance and risk need numbers; weak for quant. |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | possible | Understands working capital and cash conversion (useful in treasury/FP&A) and sanctions/trade compliance; limited for quant and bank risk. |
| Finance | strong | The default for FP&A, treasury, risk and corp dev; credible for compliance; quant roles need extra maths/programming. |
| Accounting | strong | The standard route into FP&A, controllership, treasury and financial-crime/compliance; limited for quant. |
| Marketing | stretch | Useful only in fintech growth/product roles; corporate finance and risk expect quantitative training. |
| Data Analytics | strong | SQL and analytics fit risk, transaction monitoring, fintech credit/risk and FP&A; deep quant needs more maths. |
| Economics | strong | Close match for FP&A, risk and corp dev, and a bridge to quant if combined with statistics and coding. |
| Computer Science | strong | Core route for quant developers and QR/QT at trading firms; also works for fintech and RegTech. |
| Cybersecurity | possible | Operational/technology risk and fraud in fintech; stretch for quant research. |
| Data Science | strong | Natural for risk modelling, credit scoring, bank quant, quant research and fintech analytics. |
| Artificial Intelligence | strong | Strong for quant research, bank quant and fintech credit/fraud models; thin finance knowledge must be built. |

#### (b) Matrix: background vs. role family (S = strong, P = possible, X = stretch)

| Background | 3.1 FP&A | 3.2 Treasury | 3.3 Corp dev | 3.4 Risk | 3.5 Compliance | 3.6 Bank quant | 3.7 QR/QT | 3.8 Quant dev | 3.9 Fintech biz |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Management | P | P | P | X | P | X | X | X | S |
| Logistics & Supply Chain | P | X | X | X | P | X | X | X | P |
| Finance | S | S | S | S | S | P | P | X | S |
| Accounting | S | S | P | P | S | X | X | X | P |
| Marketing | X | X | X | X | X | X | X | X | P |
| Data Analytics | P | P | X | S | S | X | X | X | S |
| Economics | S | P | S | S | P | P | P | X | S |
| Computer Science | X | X | X | P | P | P | S | S | P |
| Cybersecurity | X | X | X | P | P | X | X | X | P |
| Data Science | P | P | X | S | P | S | S | P | S |
| Artificial Intelligence | X | X | X | P | P | S | S | P | P |

Notes: Computer Science and Cybersecurity also have strong fintech engineering paths (see the Computer Science branch). A "P" for Economics in quant roles assumes a strong mathematical track; a plain economics curriculum is a stretch.
